Kept apart from Sharing profit with your team (a share of profit, not of the business); Putting money into a business (to outside backers); and Running a group (a co-op founded together). It usually starts when you plan to retire or step back, key people might leave, or the team asks for a say.
  Examples: Hand the business to the workers as you retire; Turn the business into a workers' co-op; Sell the business to your team, paid from future sales.
- **Sharing profit with your team** (money, work · changes who owns it · others, out · by consent · who waits: they wait · outcome: shared · other side: a person or household · in exchange: nothing · runs until ended, recurs · terms: what leaving costs, a claim, what you expect, where Austrians differ · other side (from the facts): Taking a share of your workplace). Card: "Your year closes on 31 March. Pay your 6 staff their share by the rule you set?"
  You do this when: You give the people who work for you a share of what the business makes, by a rule stated in advance.
  [Promise · Start · Accept · Decline · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Hand over · Pay · Receive · Withdraw · End · Pause · Resume]
  You give the people who work for you a share of what the business makes, by a rule you state in advance: a share of profit or a bonus pool. You set the share, who is in it and how it is divided, and say it before the work is done. When the period ends you show the figures it rests on and pay each person their part. Changing the rule needs the team's notice. Kept apart from Sharing ownership with your team (they own part of the business); Employing someone (a wage only); and Splitting takings (dividing what a group earned together). It usually starts when you want staff to share in a good year, when someone you want to keep might leave, or when a period ends and shares are due.
  Examples: Set a profit share before the year starts; Pay out shares at the end of a period; Show the figures the share rests on; Share profit with the team by an agreed rule.
- **Cooking together** (food · changes what it is · your group, both · by consent · who waits: nobody · outcome: nobody · runs once · terms: a signed rule binds only its signers, the remainder · no other side: none: one group, one plan). Card: "Friends are coming Sunday and everyone wants to cook. Split the dishes between you?"
  You do this when: You cook one meal with others and share it: Sunday lunch, a potluck, a cook-along.
  [Promise · Start · Accept · Decline · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Leave · Remove · Receive · Done · Withdraw · End]
  You cook one meal with others and share it: a family cooking Sunday lunch together, friends making dumplings around a table, a potluck where each brings a part, a cook-along online. You agree who makes what and who brings what, cook, and share what you made. Nobody is the host and nobody is paid. Kept apart from Cooking a meal (you cook alone); Giving and receiving food (you cook for guests); and Running a community kitchen (feeding others for a cause). It usually starts when people are coming over, when a festival needs a lot of food at once, or when friends want to learn a dish together.
  Examples: Plan who cooks what for a shared meal; Make a big festive dish together; Cook along with friends far away; Write who made a dish and what is in it; Name the dishes on a shared table; Keep the sign-up for next time.
- **Sharing a commons** (space, land and water, a permission · changes what's agreed · others, both · by consent · who waits: nobody · outcome: nobody · other side: a group · in exchange: nothing · runs until ended, recurs · terms: what leaving costs, a signed rule binds only its signers · no other side: its own other side: every member does the same act toward the others). Card: "Your water turn on the canal is Tuesday 4am to 8am. Swap it with Ruth for Thursday?"
  You do this when: You share something with others who use it under rules you all agreed: canal water, common grazing.
  [Promise · Start · Accept · Decline · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Leave · Remove · Receive · Withdraw · End · Pause · Resume]
  You share something with the others who use it, under the rules you all agreed (a commons): water turns on a canal, grazing days on common land, a fishing ground's quota, a forest you gather in. The rules say who may take how much and when, how turns are swapped, and what happens to someone who breaks them. Where the rule allows it, one turn can also be sold once, for money or for a share of the crop. You take your turn or share, swap it, sell one, give it up, and help keep the rules. When a turn ends, the next in line takes over by counting what is left. Kept apart from Sharing a place (a shelf or pitch inside one place); Sharing a machine (equipment); and Letting someone use your place (one owner says yes). It usually starts when a season or turn comes round, when someone wants to swap or buy a turn, or when a rule is broken.
  Examples: Take your turn of water; Swap a turn with another member; Give out water turns to farmers on a canal; Share out quota among boats; Report a boat fishing inside a closed zone; The guard posts the week's turns; Report blast or poison fishing; Report a net below the legal mesh.
  - Move words **Join the waiting list** (Promise). Card: "The water group has a turn free on Tuesdays from next month. Ask for it?" Asking for a share when one frees up.
  - Move words **Give up your share** (Leave). Card: "You've sold the cows. Give your grazing days back to the group?" Letting your share go back to the group.
  - Move words **Allocate a share** (Promise). Card: "18 tonnes of quota left and 6 boats. Allocate 3 tonnes each?" Giving out shares by the group's rule: plots, quota, water turns.
  - Move words **Close for a season** (Pause). Card: "The octopus are spawning. Close the reef for 3 months?" Closing a shared resource for a season, by the group's rule.
  - Move words **Report a rule breaker** (Note). Card: "Cattle grazed the closed field last night. Report it to the committee?" Telling the group someone broke its rule; what happens to them is decided together.
  - Move words **Log your turn** (Note). Card: "Your turn at the upper gate was 5 to 8 this morning. Do you tell the group how much water you got?" Closes the hole between 'you take your turn' and what happened. A tail-end farmer who lost the transplanting window finally has a dated record to back a complaint or a request to the guard.
- **Splitting takings** (money · changes who owns it · others, both · by consent · who waits: nobody · outcome: nobody · other side: a group · in exchange: nothing · runs once · terms: a signed rule binds only its signers, a shared pot · no other side: its own other side: every member does the same act toward the others). Card: "Saturday's landing is sold. By your share rule the crew get €925 each. Pay out?"
  You do this when: You and others divide what a group earned, by a rule agreed before the work. Not costs, not a whip-round.
  [Promise · Start · Accept · Decline · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Leave · Remove · Pay · Receive · Withdraw · End]
  You and others divide what a group earned: what a shared sale brought in, a boat's landing, the week's tips. The shares follow a rule agreed before the work, by hours, by points or half to the boat, and one person holds the money. When it is sold and the costs are known you work out each share and pay it. The law taxes tips. Everyone in the split sees the total it was made from (the buyer's signed sale, the pooled tips), the rule, and their own share, without having to ask. The holder counts the pot in front of the group, and each share closes when its receiver signs for it. Kept apart from Chipping in (each putting money in for one thing); Splitting costs (dividing what was spent); Sharing profit with your team (the owner choosing to give staff a share of profit); and Employing someone (a wage). It usually starts when a landing or sale is done, when the week's tips are counted, or when the stall closes after a fair.
  Examples: Split what a shared sale earns with the people who helped; Share out a catch among the crew; Share out the week's tips; Jar counted at close by two; An envelope from the host for the staff; Ask the crew leader to write down each picker's count before he pays out; Crew leader pays out a pruning crew; Write down the rule for sharing card tips; Agree a split before a shared fee comes in.
  - Move words **Share out the tips** (Pay). Card: "This week's tips are £2,140 across 9 staff by points. Send each their share?" Sharing tips by the rule; the person who holds the tips is named in advance. A service charge added to a bill is part of the price, not a tip; where both are shared, each shows as its own line.
  - Move words **Pay out the crew's shares** (Pay). Card: "Saturday's landing made €8,400 and fuel and ice cost €1,000. By your share rule the boat takes half of the €7,400 and the 4 crew get €925 each. Pay out?" Splitting a catch or harvest's earnings by shares.
- **Chipping in** (money · changes who owns it · others, both · by consent · who waits: nobody · outcome: nobody · other side: a group · holder may use it: yes, only for what was agreed, back never · in exchange: nothing · runs once · terms: held, not owned, a signed rule binds only its signers, a shared pot · no other side: its own other side: every member does the same act toward the others). Card: "Priya is leaving. Chip in $5 for her cake with your 8 colleagues in Denver?"
  You do this when: You and others each put in a small amount to buy one thing: a present, a cake, a team meal. Not dividing a bill or takings.
  [Promise · Start · Accept · Decline · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Leave · Remove · Pay · Receive · Withdraw · End]
  You and others each put in a small amount to buy one thing for someone or for the group: a leaving present, a wedding gift, a cake, a team meal. One person collects, tells everyone the amount, buys it, and shows what was spent. It ends when the thing is bought; leftover money goes back or to the person it was for, as agreed. Kept apart from Splitting takings (sharing out money you earned together); Pooling money (a lasting pot kept for the group); Contributing to a collection (a pledge recorded and returned in kind, as at a funeral); and Splitting costs (dividing a bill already paid). It usually starts when a colleague leaves, a birthday or wedding comes, or the team finishes a hard shift.
  Examples: Chip in with colleagues or friends for a cake, meal or treat for someone's occasion; Put in for a gift for a friend or neighbour; Put in for a team meal; Put in for a leaving present.
  - Move words **Put in your share** (Pay). Card: "Priya's leaving cake: $5 each. Put in yours?" Your part of the whip-round.
- **Going into partnership** (money, equipment, rights and claims · changes who owns it + what's agreed · others, both · by consent · who waits: nobody · outcome: shared · other side: a person or household · price: a formula · closes when every party says yes · in exchange: a share of the outcome · runs until ended · terms: a signed price, what leaving costs, a claim, what you expect, the wait, the law's floor, tax on a price, what's taken off, where it came from · no other side: self: every partner signs the same terms). Card: "You and Nasra want to run the Saturday stall together. Write who puts in what and how you share it?"
  You do this when: You and others start a business or job together and write down what each puts in, the shares, and who loses what if it fails.
  [Promise · Start · Accept · Decline · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Hand over · Pay · Receive · Withdraw · End · Pause · Resume]
  You and one or more people start a business or a job together and write down what each puts in (money, a van, a kitchen, land, your work), what share of the profit each gets, when it ends or how a partner leaves, and, because it can go badly, who loses what if it does. A quiet backer can sign too: she puts in money for a share, and the most she can lose is a sum she names. Each partner is bound only by what she signed. A promise of work binds only what she put up, never her person, and leaving is always open, at the cost she agreed up front. Where your terms say nothing about a loss, the card says 'not agreed' and fills nothing in. Kept apart from Putting money into a business (you put money into someone else's business and do not run it); Sharing ownership with your team (a share for the people who work there); and Having a business of your own (a business of your own). It usually starts when two people want to run a stall or a farm together, when a friend offers money for a share, or when a business has grown too big for one.
  Examples: Start a stall together; Add a quiet backer; Say how it ends.
- **Spending a group's fair-trade premium** (money · changes who owns it + what's agreed · your group, both · by consent · who waits: nobody · outcome: nobody · runs until ended, recurs · terms: a signed rule binds only its signers, the remainder, a shared pot · no other side: none: one group, one plan). Card: "Buyers paid $0.20 a pound extra on 31,000 pounds of coffee this year. Spend the $6,200 premium on the washing station roof?"
  You do this when: A group holds a premium that is the group's, and spends it by its own rule and vote.
  [Promise · Start · Accept · Decline · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Leave · Remove · Pay · Receive · Withdraw · End · Pause · Resume]
  You and your group hold and spend a fair-trade premium: a fair-trade or similar scheme makes buyers pay a premium on top of the price, and the premium goes to the group of growers or workers, not to each person. The group holds it in its own account and spends it by a rule it wrote and every member agreed to: a vote at a general meeting on what to spend it on (a school, a well, a processing machine, a loan fund, a bonus), a committee that keeps the books, and a report back to every member showing what came in, what was spent and what is left. Some schemes require a share to go to the community or to workers rather than the owners, and a certifier checks the spending. It is the group's money, held for the group, so no one member or buyer may spend it alone; keep it apart from the group's ordinary money, and keep the receipts. Kept apart from Pooling money (a standing pot the group chose to fill itself); Getting or giving a certificate (the mark that makes buyers pay it); and Receiving a patronage refund (a surplus returned by use). It usually starts when a sale with a premium is paid, the year's premium is known, or a general meeting is called to decide how to spend it.
  Examples: Vote on how to spend it; Show the books to every member; Keep the premium apart; Pass a share to workers.

## Doing

### Create
- **Developing a product** (an idea · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: she waits · outcome: her · runs until ended · terms: what you expect, the wait). Card: "Your test batch of chilli jam sold out at the feira at R$22. Add it to the range?"
  You do this when: You add something new to sell: name it, say what is in it, try it on people.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · End · Pause · Resume]
  You add something new to sell: name it, say what is in it, and try it on people first. It could be a new plant variety or a jam you first made in small batches. You say what you expect (how many, by when) and the card shows what it takes from what you sell now: stall space, cooking hours, ingredients. You start it, change it, invite people to taste it, and stop it for good if you drop it. A new version that keeps the original is a new product. What you create is yours unless a contract you signed at work says otherwise. Kept apart from Developing a recipe (the way to make it); Setting what, how much and where you sell (what is on your list); and Setting prices (what it costs). It usually starts when a test batch sells out, an ingredient gets dearer, a customer asks for something, or a season brings a new crop.
  Examples: Create a new dish and try it on people before it goes on the menu; Develop a product together with a producer; Launch a product from trial batches; Make a variation of a product that keeps the original.
  - Move words **Change a product** (Change or End). Card: "Your flapjack's oats went up 20%. Change it to the cheaper oats, same taste test?" Change what is in it, how it looks, or how it is packed, or stop making it. Its price is Setting prices; which products you sell is Setting what you sell.
- **Developing a recipe** (an idea · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: nobody · outcome: nobody · runs until ended). Card: "You've cooked this curry 4 times with no recipe. Save it as yours?"
  You do this when: You write down a new dish or settle an old one into a version anyone could cook. Not what you sell.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · End · Pause · Resume]
  You write down a new dish, or settle Gran's stew into one version that anyone could cook. A process counts too, such as a brew schedule or a cure. You try it, note what you change, and invite others to taste. Saving it as yours says you made it. Whether others may cook it is for you and them to agree. A version that keeps the original (a vegan one) is a new recipe. It is yours unless a contract you signed at work says otherwise. Kept apart from Developing a product (what you sell); Letting someone use your recipe or brand (letting others use it); and Cooking a meal (tonight's cooking). It usually starts when you have cooked the same thing from memory several times, a relative is getting older, a dish needs one fixed version for a team, or you want a variation.
  Examples: Save a recipe as yours; Write a recipe down as the one version everyone follows; Make a vegan version and keep the original.
  - Move words **Change a recipe** (Change or End). Card: "You've added 5 minutes three times now. Change the recipe to 45?" Edit it, add notes ('less salt'), or retire it; it stays in your history and can come back. A one-off swap tonight is Cooking a meal. A version that keeps the original is Developing a recipe.
- **Publishing food content** (an idea · changes what's known · others, out · by consent · who waits: nobody · outcome: nobody · other side: anyone · in exchange: nothing · runs until ended · terms: what leaving costs, done when read · no other side: whoever reads it answers with Receive, the reading itself). Card: "Your reel on sourdough scoring is ready. Post it?"
  You do this when: You make things about food that people see again and again, for free: videos, a newsletter, a book.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · End · Pause · Resume]
  You make things about food that people see again and again, for free: videos, photos, a cookbook, a family recipe book, a newsletter, casual posts of your dinner. You start it, change it, and stop or pause it. Money comes through other acts: a sponsor in Taking a sponsorship, subscribers in Selling subscriptions, a guide in Letting someone use your recipe or brand, each marked as paid wherever it shows. Your followers are yours. Kept apart from Sharing a recipe (giving one recipe to people); Posting an offer or a want (your goods, not food content); and Having a business of your own (a firm). It usually starts when you want to show what you cook, when an audience asks, or when a season gives you something to film.
  Examples: Make videos, photos, writing or a cookbook about food; Turn your recipes into a booklet or cards to give or sell; Collect recipes from family or friends into one shared book.
  - Move words **Change food content** (Change or End). Card: "Your pinned reel still shows last year's price. Update it to the new one?" Edit, update or take down a post, video, cookbook page or newsletter.
  - Move words **Ask for a copy to be taken down** (Question). Card: "A page shows your cake photo three weeks after you posted it. Ask the platform to take it down?" Asking a platform or a page owner to remove a copy of your photo, recipe or video, with your own dated post as the proof. Their answer is their word.
  - Move words **Invite followers to your own list** (Invite). Card: "All your followers reach you through one app. Invite them to your own newsletter or message group?" Inviting the people who follow you on a platform to reach you directly, by email or a group of your own. Only those who say yes are added, and the list is yours.
- **Running a group** (people · changes what's agreed · others, both · by consent · who waits: they wait · outcome: shared · other side: a group · price: a rule she signed · in exchange: money · runs until ended, recurs · terms: a signed price, credit, what backs it, what leaving costs, a claim, what you expect, a signed rule binds only its signers, the law's floor, tax on a price, what's taken off, a shared pot · no other side: its own other side: every member does the same act toward the others). Card: "You and 5 neighbours have bought rice together 4 times. Make it a buying group?"
  You do this when: You start a group that lasts, or one for a single job: a household, a co-op, a chama, a machinery ring.
  [Promise · Start · Accept · Decline · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Leave · Remove · Pay · Receive · Withdraw · End · Pause · Resume]
  You start a group that lasts, or one for a single job: a household, a co-op, a lunch club, a chama, a machinery ring, a caterer's crew for one wedding. You set its rules: who holds the money and how many must sign, who does which job, what members pay, how people join, get fined and leave. Then you run it: collect dues, show members the accounts, share out what is left as the rule says, and merge it, split it or close it. Members can be people or other groups. A machine is shared by the group but is never a member. Closing a group pays what it owes first, then gives back what each put in, then shares the rest. The count of the box is signed by two members, the second named by the group's rule, so a shortfall never rests on one person's word. Kept apart from Taking part in a group (your side, as a member); Deciding as a group (the vote itself); and Having a business of your own (a firm of its own). It usually starts when neighbours keep buying the same thing, a job needs a crew, a shared kitchen or machine is wanted, or a year closes.
  Examples: Start a household, co-op, club or savings group; Start a machinery ring with neighbours; Start a buying group; Ask members for a fee to belong; Start a shared food kitty for a household.
  - Move words **Add or remove a member** (Invite or Remove). Card: "Tom has moved out and owes £14 for the shared shop. Settle his share and take him off the list?" The group's side. Your own joining or leaving is Taking part in a group. A machine is never a member: to connect a drone to your farm, add it and let it act for you, in one tap.
  - Move words **Set a member's limit** (Change). Card: "Rosa's bakery sells €3,000 a month in the circle. Let her owe the circle up to €300?" How far a member may go into debt with the group, set by its rule.
  - Move words **Elect or appoint someone** (Invite). Card: "The yearly meeting is Saturday and the treasurer's term ends. Put Ama forward for treasurer?" Giving a role to someone for a term, by the group's rule; the role outlives whoever holds it.
  - Move words **Hand over the role** (Pass). Card: "Ama takes over as treasurer. Hand over the books, the keys and the bank mandate?" Passing a role, its records and its keys to the next holder.
  - Move words **Collect dues** (Promise). Card: "12 members haven't paid this month's dues. Send reminders?" Asking members for their regular dues.
  - Move words **Fine a member** (Promise). Card: "Juma has missed 3 work days this season. Fine him KSh 500 under the rule?" A fine under the group's own rule; it binds members only.
  - Move words **Share out what is left** (Pay). Card: "The signed accounts show £18,000 left after costs. Your rule shares it by hours worked: £2,340 to you. Agree?" Paying back what the group earned, by what each member supplied, bought or worked, as its rule says; never per head unless the rule says so.
  - Move words **Present the accounts** (Note). Card: "The yearly meeting is in 2 weeks. Send members the accounts, checked by someone outside the group?" Showing members the accounts, checked by someone outside the group. It is done when members have read them.
  - Move words **Merge with another group** (Change). Card: "The two chamas vote on joining into one. Merge, with both pots and all members?" Two groups joining; each decides by its own rule, then members, things and open deals pass across.
  - Move words **Split off** (Start). Card: "The north village wants its own water group. Split off with its share of the fund?" Part of a group forming its own, with its share of members and things.
  - Move words **Wind up and share out** (End). Card: "Hill St is ending: the fridge, the £240 kitty and the shared rice. Pay what's owed, then split the rest?" Ending a group or a household: pay debts first, then share out the pot and shared things by the rule; copies of shared records go to everyone.
- **Having a business of your own** (people, a permission · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: she waits · outcome: her · runs until ended · terms: what you expect, the wait). Card: "You've sold mandazi 40 days running. Registering costs KSh 1,000 and a yearly return, and opens bigger buyers and a loan. Register, or keep trading as you are?"
  You do this when: You make a food business of its own apart from your household, and later end it: starting is its first move and closing its last.
  Also called: Starting a business; Closing a business.
  Was: act-e9567868c4.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · End · Pause · Resume]
  You make a food business of its own, apart from your household: its name, its own money, and who answers if it owes (you, or only the business). Registering, licences and tax numbers are steps the law requires, each its own act; the card shows what they cost and give, and where your place lets a small trader sell without them. Closing a business: You end a food business of its own: pay what it owes first, end open deals on their terms, file the last returns, and what is left is yours. Everyone who may be owed is invited to state their claim in their own signed word, so the list is built from claims, not from your memory. Kept apart from Opening another site (a new place for a business you run); Putting money into a business (putting money in); Running a group (a group); Selling your business (someone else takes it on); and Paying what you can to everyone you owe (when you cannot pay everyone). It usually starts when you have sold steadily for weeks, a bank or buyer asks for a business, or a partner wants to join; as Closing a business, when sales stop covering costs, you retire, or you decide to stop.
  Examples: Trade under a name of your own; Choose who answers for the business's debts; Tell the tax office you now trade; Close a business and settle what it owes; Stop trading and file the last returns; Sell the equipment to pay what is owed; Closing a business.
- **Opening another site** (space, land and water, people · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: she waits · outcome: her · runs once · terms: what you expect, the wait, the remainder). Card: "Your Jakarta delivery orders have doubled in 6 months. A small kitchen for delivery only is Rp 15 million a month on a 2-year lease. Open it?"
  You do this when: You open another place for a business you already run: a branch, a kiosk, a dark kitchen.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Done · End]
  You open another place for a business you already run: a branch, a kiosk in another town, a dark kitchen for deliveries. It brings together renting the place, the fit-out, the licences, the staff and what you will sell there. You say what you expect it to take and by when, and the card shows the rent, the fit-out, the loan, what leaving the lease early costs, and your other choices. Licences are their own acts, because the law requires them. Kept apart from Having a business of your own (a new business); Building or improving a place (work on a place you have); Selling at a stall, a gate or a screen (another pitch on market day); and Letting someone use your recipe or brand (someone else opens under your name). It usually starts when you sell out every day, customers come from far away, a good place comes free, or a second shift could use the same team.
  Examples: Open a branch where customers come from; Open a delivery kitchen; Open a branch in a second town.

### Make
- **Building or improving a place** (space, land and water, energy · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: she waits · outcome: her · runs once · terms: what you expect, the wait, the remainder). Card: "Paddock 4 floods every March. Lay a drain in August before the ewes go out?"
  You do this when: You build or improve a place where food is made, kept or grown: a building, a borehole, a polytunnel.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Done · End]
  You build or improve the places where food is made, kept or grown: a building, a drain, a borehole, a road, a kitchen fit-out, a polytunnel, solar panels, a biogas digester. It adds something that lasts, once. You plan the work, do it, invite helpers, and finish it. The card shows the other ways to get there (a builder, renting the capacity). On rented land, the lease says who owns the improvement. Work that touches a neighbour or a shared source needs their yes. Kept apart from Keeping equipment and places in order (upkeep); Doing or getting a job for pay (paying a builder); and Making a batch (food, not the place). It usually starts when a place floods or breaks, a season is about to start, an order needs more room, or energy costs rise.
  Examples: Build or fix a building, fence, drain or water point; Fit out a kitchen, stall or polytunnel; Put up solar panels for the cold room.
- **Making a batch** (food · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: she waits · outcome: her · runs once, recurs · terms: what you expect, the wait, the remainder). Card: "Line 2 runs the nut bar at 2pm. Run the nut-free oat bar first and save a deep clean?"
  You do this when: You make a run ahead to sell or serve, from a home kitchen to a factory, for a market you expect. Not one thing for one customer who has ordered it.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Done · End · Pause · Resume]
  You make a run ahead to sell or serve, from a home kitchen to a factory: 50 pies for Saturday's market, 300 school lunches, a morning's smoked fish, or a kitchen's prep before service. You decide what to make and in what order, start the run, write down which lots went in (a photo of the bag or crate label is enough), check it as it goes, write down what you find, and weigh or count what came out. It is done when the batch is ready to sell, or thrown out with the loss written down. A batch can wait until someone says it is fine to sell. A batch for an order is made for that order. A batch for stock is made on what you expect to sell, and the gap is yours. Kept apart from Making to order (one order made as it comes in); Cooking ahead (a household cooks for later); and Preserving food (keeping a glut). It usually starts when a market or school order is booked, the day's bookings are known, a key item runs low, a catch comes in, or a line is scheduled.
  Examples: Bake a batch to sell at the weekend market; Cook a batch of school lunches; Smoke a morning's catch to sell; Make tomorrow's prep list from orders and bookings; Make more of a base that is running low before service; Label and date everything that was prepped; Use yesterday's prep first; Record a batch reading: espresso dose, yield and time; Sausage or mince run from trim; Mix a batch of layer feed.
  - Move words **Pack and label** (Note). Card: "40 jars sealed. Print labels with lot 0912 and best-before March?" Weigh, fill, seal and label each run with date, lot code and allergens.
  - Move words **Hold it back** (Pause). Card: "Batch 12 tested at 4.2, lower than usual. Hold it until the lab result?" Keep something back from going out until someone says it is fine: a batch on hold until a test, milk or eggs inside a treatment's waiting days, an animal not yet clear to sell. Release it when the result or the date comes.
  - Move words **Release a batch** (Resume). Card: "The lab result for batch 12 is back clean. Release it to sell?" The batch is checked and you let it go on sale.
  - Move words **Sign off the batch** (Done). Card: "The robot line made 400 meals today. Sign off the batch?" A machine of yours made the batch, so you check it and put your name to it: your machine is your hands, and you answer for what it made. Anything you find goes with your signature, or you send it back.
  - Move words **Rework it** (Change). Card: "Batch 2411 came out thin at the 10 o'clock reading and is on hold. Put it back through the cooker, sell it as a second, or throw it out?" A held batch put back through the process, sold as a second, or thrown out, with the new batch tied to the old one.
  - Move words **Keep a sample** (Note). Card: "You are cooking for 200 at the wedding. Freeze a small portion of each dish, dated, for a week?" A small portion of what you served, frozen with its lot and date and kept for a while. If someone falls ill, it shows what was served and lets an honest cook answer blame she could not otherwise answer. It is evidence, not a hold on sale.
- **Making to order** (food · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: nobody · outcome: nobody · runs once · terms: the remainder). Card: "Table 4 wants a flat white and a nut-free brownie from the labelled tin. Start the order?"
  You do this when: You make one thing for one customer as the order comes in. Not a run made ahead for a market.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Hand over · Done · End]
  You make one thing for one customer as the order comes in: a plate at a table, a butcher's special cut, a baker's custom cake. You start the order, make it as the customer asked (ingredients, size, allergies), and hand it over. It ends if the customer calls it off. Who bears the cost of what is already made is agreed in the order itself. Which order goes first on a busy line is Running a shift. Kept apart from Making a batch (a run made all at once); Cooking a meal (your own meal); and Buying and selling food to use (the sale). It usually starts when an order arrives or a customer asks for something made just for them.
  Examples: Make and plate a dish to order for a customer; Make a custom cake for a customer's date; Cut a special order for a customer; A live pick, killed and dressed to order; A live pick, killed and dressed to order; A live pick, killed and dressed to order; Quote from cakes you sold before.
  - Move words **Hand over an allergy plate** (Hand over). Card: "Table 8 has a nut allergy on the order. Who is making the plate, and who is carrying it to the table?" The care for an allergy is physical (a clean board, a hand-to-hand pass) and leaves nothing behind. Two signed words, the cook's and the server's, are the business's proof and each worker's own record of doing it right.
  - Move words **Rework it** (Change). Card: "Table 6 sent the risotto back: too salty. Remake it and note it against the ticket?" A held batch put back through the process, sold as a second, or thrown out, with the new batch tied to the old one.
- **Slaughtering an animal** (living things · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: nobody · outcome: nobody · runs once, recurs · terms: the remainder). Card: "The 6 cockerels reach killing weight this week. Slaughter Saturday with the freezer cleared, or keep them another week?"
  You do this when: You turn a living animal into a carcass: at home, the abattoir or as a rite.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Done · End · Pause · Resume]
  You turn a living animal into a carcass: at home, on the farm, at the abattoir, or as a rite with others (qurbani on Eid al-Adha split in thirds, a family matanza, a hangi). You fix the day and place, bring in helpers, write down what was done (the count and tags, who cut and by which rite, what the inspector said, and anything held until a result comes), and it ends at the carcass. Cutting it up is Preparing food for sale, and keeping it is Preserving food. Giving a third away is Giving and receiving food; paying someone else to do it is Having food processed. Kept apart from Preparing food for sale (a carcass already food); and Preserving food (it starts with food). It usually starts when animals reach killing weight, a festival or family tradition is near, or the season turns cold enough to hang meat.
  Examples: Slaughter for a festival; Kill and pluck birds at home; Butcher a pig together; Prepare an animal for a shared feast in the old way.
  - Move words **Note the inspector's call** (Note). Card: "The inspector has looked at the eleven carcasses. What did he say?" What the inspector decided about each carcass: passed, held, or part or all condemned and why. It goes on the kill record the owner sees.
  - Move words **Hold the carcass** (Pause). Card: "A sample is at the lab for these four pigs. Hold the carcasses until the result is back?" Hold and Release already exist for a batch. A carcass held for a result is the same move, and today the hold is a chalk X on a hook with no name and no time.
- **Grading produce** (food · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: nobody · outcome: nobody · runs once, recurs · terms: the remainder). Card: "This week's tomatoes sort at 60% grade A. Sort the rest for the sauce maker?"
  You do this when: You sort, grade, clean and weigh what was harvested or caught before it is sold. Not repacking or cutting it.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Done · End · Pause · Resume]
  You sort, grade, clean and weigh what was harvested or caught, before it is sold: mangoes into export, local and juice, fish by size for the auction, teff threshed and winnowed. You start the sort, write down the weights and grades, bring in helpers, and pass the lots on. It repeats with each harvest or landing. A buyer's grade and yours can differ, so say which standard you graded by. Kept apart from Making a batch (a new product); Preserving food (keeping a glut); Preparing food for sale (small packs); and Storing a harvest (drying and holding). It usually starts when a harvest or catch arrives, or when a buyer names the grades they want.
  Examples: Sort and grade a harvest before you sell it; Size the catch before the auction; Winnow and clean grain before it is weighed; Say where the graded-out produce goes; Sort eggs by size and set cracked and dirty ones apart before you sell; Machine rejects; Decide what the cosmetic rejects become.
- **Cooking a meal** (food · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: nobody · outcome: nobody · runs once · terms: the remainder). Card: "You've got eggs, spinach and rice going off Friday. Make egg fried rice tonight?"
  You do this when: You cook a meal now, from what you have or a recipe, for yourself and yours. Not food made now to eat later.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Done · End]
  You cook a meal, from what you have or from a recipe: tonight's dinner, a dish you try again with changes, a lunch for a crowd. You start, change the plan as you go, write down what you did, pass a job on, and finish with the meal done. The card shows what each way uses (time, what is used up) next to a takeaway. Allergies are cooked around first. Kept apart from Cooking together (others cook with you); Making to order (a customer's order); Making a batch (a run made to sell); and Cooking ahead (food for later). It usually starts when food is nearing its date, it is evening and nothing is planned, guests are coming, or someone sets a theme night.
  Examples: Turn what is going off or left over into a meal; Cook a meal that fits the time and energy you have tonight; Cook one base meal and finish it differently for each person's needs or tastes; Plan a cooking timeline back from serving time for a big meal; Say what went into the pot.
  - Move words **Put the rest away** (Note). Card: "The dal was for six and four of you ate. Put the rest in the fridge, or freeze it?" Cooking a meal done with more made than eaten: the leftover becomes a line in stock with the day it was cooked.
- **Cooking ahead** (food · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: she waits · outcome: nobody · runs once, recurs · terms: the wait, the remainder). Card: "Sunday's chilli makes plenty. Freeze the extra for the week?"
  You do this when: You cook now so that food is ready later: a big pot on Sunday, portions in the freezer. Not tonight's dinner.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Done · End · Pause · Resume]
  You cook now so that food is ready later: a big pot on Sunday, portions in the freezer, packed lunches, a slow step like soaking beans the night before, or a few easy meals for nights you cannot cook. It repeats weekly for many people. Kept apart from Making a batch (it is a run to sell); Cooking a meal (food made to eat tonight); and Preserving food (you turn a glut into jars or stores). It usually starts when it is a quiet time such as Sunday afternoon, you know a busy week is coming, or a recipe needs a slow step.
  Examples: Cook a big batch ahead and keep portions for later; Pack a lunch for yourself or someone; Start the slow step of a recipe ahead; Keep a few easy meals ready for nights you cannot cook.
- **Preserving food** (food · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: she waits · outcome: her · runs once, recurs · terms: what you expect, the wait, the remainder). Card: "12 kg of tomatoes are ripe at once. Bottle passata on Saturday?"
  You do this when: You turn a glut into food that keeps: jam, pickles, cured or dried fish.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Done · End · Pause · Resume]
  You turn a glut into food that keeps, to eat or sell later: jam, pickles, kimchi, passata, cured ham, smoked or dried fish and fruit. You start, change the method, write down the date and batch, bring in helpers, and mark it done. It repeats each season and waits for the ferment or cure. The card shows the days, the racks and the jars it takes, next to eating or selling it fresh. Drying a crop in its own form is Storing a harvest, and years of ageing are Ageing food or drink. You say how much you started with and how many jars you ended with, and a batch that went bad is written as lost. Kept apart from Making a batch (a product made to sell now); Cooking ahead (food for the next few days); and Keeping food fresh (food kept as it is). It usually starts when many things ripen at once, a price is low at the market, or a ferment is ready to jar.
  Examples: Preserve food by pickling, drying, curing or fermenting; Turn a glut into jars; Dry the catch or harvest for the lean months; Cure and smoke meat for the winter; Feed or rest a starter.
- **Preparing food for sale** (food · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: nobody · outcome: her · runs once, recurs · terms: what you expect, the remainder). Card: "46 quarter-kilo sugar bags sold this week, 6 left. Bag up the new 50 kg sack tonight?"
  You do this when: You repack or cut food before it is sold: small packs, joints and fillets; what you do is the fact. Not grading it, not killing an animal, not a new product.
  Also called: Bagging up food to sell; Cutting meat or fish.
  Was: act-ecbab4f069.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Done · End · Pause · Resume]
  You break a sack, tin or carton into small packs a customer can afford: a quarter-kilo of sugar, oil in small bags, dal in 250 g packets, single eggs. You start, choose the pack size, count the packs, and mark it done. It repeats whenever the small packs run out. You say how much went in and how many packs came out, so spillage and short measure show. Cutting meat or fish: You break a carcass or a whole fish into cuts: trimming, jointing, filleting, gutting, as a butcher, a fishmonger or a home cook with a whole chicken. You choose the cuts, do the cutting, write down what you got (the weight of each cut, and of the trim, bone and fat), and which carcass or lot it came from, so a cut can be followed back, and mark it done. It repeats with every delivery. Kept apart from Making a batch (it makes a product, not repacks one); Grading produce (it sorts by quality); and Slaughtering an animal (the animal is alive, not yet food). It usually starts when the small packs on the shelf are nearly gone, when a new sack arrives, or when payday brings customers who buy small and often; as Cutting meat or fish, when a whole carcass or catch arrives, stock of a popular cut runs low, or a busy day such as a weekend or festival is ahead.
  Examples: Bag up a sack into small packs to sell; Repack oil or flour into the sizes people buy; Sell single items from a larger pack; Make up small portions of spices; Heaps and tins in your own unit; Count the packs from a sack; Count the pouches from a drum; Butcher or prepare meat or fish; Gut and fillet the catch; Cut a carcass to match what sells; Break down a whole fish for sale; Cutting meat or fish.
- **Making compost** (by-products · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: she waits · outcome: nobody · runs once, recurs · terms: the wait, the remainder). Card: "Your bin in Leeds has been turning for 8 weeks and reads 18 degrees. Turn it today, or leave it to finish?"
  You do this when: You turn your own scraps and manure into compost for your own beds.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Done · End · Pause · Resume]
  You turn your own food scraps, manure and green waste into compost for your own beds or plot, or tend a heap that neighbours or a garden group bring their scraps to. It can be a heap, a bin, a wormery or a bokashi bucket. You add scraps and dry material, turn the pile, watch the heat, and write down when it will be ready to use. Kept apart from Getting rid of waste (that sends it away, this keeps it); Selling and buying in bulk (that sells it on); and Growing crops (that uses the finished compost). It usually starts when the scrap bucket is full, the heap has gone cold, or a bed is bare and waiting.
  Examples: Turn a heap that has gone cold; Bury a sealed bokashi bucket; Start a worm bin for a small home.
- **Brewing** (food · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: she waits · outcome: her · runs until ended, recurs · terms: what you expect, the wait, the remainder). Card: "Your ginger beer in Kingston is two days into its first ferment. Bottle it tomorrow?"
  You do this when: You make a drink that needs time to ferment: beer, wine, cider, kombucha.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · End · Pause · Resume]
  You make a drink that needs time to ferment: beer, wine, cider, kombucha, ginger beer, palm wine, kvass. You mix it, let it work, taste it, bottle or barrel it, and wait. A batch started from a culture kept from an earlier one (yeast, a mother, a starter) names the batch it came from, so the chain can be followed. A batch closes with the bottled quantity counted against what went in; where your place's rules relieve duty on lost drink, a recorded loss lowers what is owed. Kept apart from Preserving food (food, not a drink); Ageing food or drink (it starts after the drink is made); and Making a batch (a run made to sell on a schedule). It usually starts when the fruit or grain is in, a bucket is free, or the season for it comes.
  Examples: Start a ferment; Bottle when it is ready; Tap a drink at the right time.
  - Move words **Bottle or barrel it** (Note). Card: "The elderflower fizz has worked for 9 days and you have 40 clean bottles in the shed. Do you want to bottle it tomorrow?" This is the moment drink becomes stock. No receipt exists for it.

### Grow
- **Growing crops** (food · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: she waits · outcome: her · runs once, recurs · terms: what you expect, the wait, the remainder). Card: "Frost at 3am, −2°C, and your seedlings are outside. Cover the seedlings tonight?"
  You do this when: You grow plants for food within a season: sow, tend, protect, harvest.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Hand over · Done · End · Pause · Resume]
  You grow plants for food, in a pot, a garden, a field, a shared plot, a tunnel or a lit indoor room: sowing, tending, protecting and harvesting, season after season. Seed and work go in now for food later, so when you sow you note what you expect (yield, price, date) and set the harvest beside it. Each sowing notes the seed lot, so a poor lot can be traced to the dealer; each picking notes its weight in your own measure; what you find walking the field (pests, disease, hail, flood, frost, ripeness) is noted with the date and a photo, your proof of when you knew. Whoever does a job in the field, a hired hand, a neighbour on a swap day or your own family, has it on their own record too. A spray's record and harvest wait may be required by law. A shared plot shares its harvest by the group's rule. When the harvest is in you say where it went: sold, eaten at home, given away, spoiled or left in the field. A neighbour or someone in the family who worked a day can ask you to sign it, and it stays on their record. Kept apart from Growing trees and vines (years before the first crop); Raising animals (animals); and Planning what to grow or feed (the plan, before any seed goes in). It usually starts when sowing time or the last frost comes, a weather warning arrives, a pest is found, or a bed is left empty.
  Examples: Grow a crop in a pot, garden or field; Protect plants from frost or heat; Fix soil that is poor, dry, waterlogged or tired; Use empty land for something now: a quick crop, a cover crop, or rent it out; Start seeds indoors before the season; Use finished compost on a bed; Harvest food, said in her own measure; Record nitrogen in splits; Tie seaweed cuttings to the lines; Harvest food in her own measures; Check a spray's waiting days against a booked harvest; Clear a bay or bed between crops.
  - Move words **Harvest food** (Note or Done). Card: "Block 3 apples hit full sweetness and rain comes Thursday. Send the pickers Tuesday?" Picking or cutting a crop when it is ready: each picking is a Note with its weights; Done is the last harvest of the crop. Grading is Grading produce; milk, eggs, honey and shearing are words on Raising animals.
  - Move words **Sow or plant** (Note). Card: "Soil is warm enough and rain is due Thursday. Sow the beans in block 2 on Wednesday?" Seed, rate, block and date, each time.
  - Move words **Water, feed or spray** (Note). Card: "Block 2 tomatoes were sprayed with copper on Tuesday. Safe to pick from the 5th. Book the pickers then?" Product, dose, block and weather; the harvest interval starts here. A spray keeps the label's waiting days before picking and before people go back in, so a booked pick or crew is checked against them.
  - Move words **Weed or scout** (Note). Card: "Fall armyworm found in 3 rows of the maize. Spray the patch today?" What was found walking the field: pests, disease, weeds.
  - Move words **Save seed** (Note). Card: "Your beans set well. Save 200 g for next year and the seed library?" Keeping seed from this crop to plant again or share.
  - Move words **Share out the harvest** (Hand over). Card: "The community garden's beans are in: 40 kg for 12 members. Share it out by work days?" When a crop is grown together, sharing what grew, by the rule.
  - Move words **Tell the neighbours** (Note). Card: "You spray the orchard on Thursday morning. Tell the 3 beekeepers nearby by Tuesday?" Telling neighbours ahead of something you plan that affects them: what, where and when. They can ask questions or ask you to change it, and you see who has read it.
  - Move words **Work the ground** (Note). Card: "Ploughing on Field 3 stopped at dusk. Record how much of the 22 hectares is done?" Ploughing, discing, cultivating or rolling: the field, the tool, the hectares done, who drove, the date. Drilling is Sow or plant; a pass that sprays or spreads is Water, feed or spray.
  - Move words **Protect from the weather** (Note). Card: "Frost is forecast tonight and 12 tomato plants went out on Saturday. You have 2 sheets of fleece. Cover them, bring them in, or leave them?" Covering, shading, netting, heating or watering a crop against frost, hail or heat, with what you did, where, when and what it used.
  - Move words **Map your plot** (Note). Card: "A buyer wants to know where this cocoa was grown. Walk the edge of the plot with your phone?" Walking the edge of a plot with your phone to record where it is. A buyer may ask for it as proof of where food grew; it stays yours until you share it. Registering the land is a longer act of its own.
- **Raising animals** (living things · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: she waits · outcome: her · runs until ended, recurs · terms: what you expect, the wait, the remainder). Card: "This hen is hunched and not eating; two others look fine. Call the vet if she is worse by tomorrow?"
  You do this when: You look after animals day to day: feed, water, check, treat, from hens to a hive.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Hand over · End · Pause · Resume]
  You look after animals day to day: feeding, watering, checking, treating and protecting them, from hens and sheep to a hive, a fish pond or a shellfish lease, with milking, collecting eggs, shearing, taking honey and harvesting fish as steps noted with their yield. You start the care on the day the animals arrive, with how many and from where, count them at the evening round, change the care as the animals change, note each job, invite a vet or helper, and end it when the animals go. Fish and shellfish cannot be counted alive, so their number is what you stocked less what you counted dead, checked by your samples and your harvest. Milk, eggs, honey or fish from a treated animal are held until their date. Selling them is Selling animals, sending them away is Moving animals, and an order in an outbreak is Following an official order. At the round you say where each missing animal went: sold, eaten, given, died or strayed. Kept apart from Looking after someone's animals (animals that are not yours); Breeding animals (parents and births); Looking after a pet (not for food or sale); Moving animals (to another place); Slaughtering an animal; and Breeding plants and saving seed (plants, not animals). It usually starts when there is a daily round, a sick animal, a change in yield, a season such as shearing, a pond or lease to stock, or a hot spell, a cold night or a power cut puts the animals at risk.
  Examples: Feed, water, check and groom your animals every day; Treat or protect animals; Spot an animal or hive that is unwell and decide whether to call the vet; Hold food from sale until it is safe to sell; Send animals to market or a buyer at the right time and weight; Set a feeding table for fish by size and water; Record a treatment: the dip tank and the village vaccinator; Something wrong with one animal; Write down the feed you put in each pond; Fish in a tank; Act on a heat reading in the shed.
  - Move words **Milk** (Note). Card: "Daisy gave 4 litres less than her week's average. Call the vet?" Each milking, with its yield; twice a day, mostly by machine.
  - Move words **Collect eggs** (Note). Card: "38 eggs today, 6 fewer than usual. Check the north house for a broody hen?" Each day's count, cracked ones apart. Floor, hedge and cracked eggs are counted apart, by house if you keep more than one, since a hidden nest's eggs are of unknown age.
  - Move words **Shear** (Note). Card: "The shearer can come on the 12th for 120 sheep. Book him?" Once or twice a year, often by a hired shearer; the clip weighed.
  - Move words **Take honey** (Note). Card: "The supers on hive 3 are capped. Take the honey this weekend?" Taking honey from the hives, leaving enough for the bees.
  - Move words **Record a treatment** (Note). Card: "Cow 88 had antibiotics on Monday. Hold her milk until the 14th?" Medicine given, dose and withdrawal period, in the medicine book the law asks for. The entry carries the day the medicine's waiting time ends, from the label or the vet's signed word, so any sale, delivery or harvest of that animal's milk, meat, eggs or fish before then shows it.
  - Move words **Record a birth** (Note). Card: "Naserian calved last night, a bull calf. Note the birth for his papers?" A birth in your herd or flock: mother, how many, sex, alive or dead, any tag. It fills the births report the law asks for and the young animal's papers.
  - Move words **Dry off** (Note). Card: "Rosie is due in nine weeks and is giving seven litres. Do you dry her off today or milk on a few more days? Which dry-cow treatment do you use?" One tap on a cow. Milk stops being expected; the dry-cow treatment goes to Record a treatment with its date.
  - Move words **Take a harvest** (Hand over). Card: "The tilapia in pond 3 are the size your buyer in Kisumu asked for. Draw the pond down Thursday and call the crew?" Taking fish, shrimp or shellfish out of a pond, cage or lease, all at once or in part: the weight, the count per kilo or the size, what you kept and who took the rest. Sorting by size is Grading produce, and the sale is Selling in bulk or the market you sell at. The last harvest of a stocking closes it.
  - Move words **Take in a swarm** (Note). Card: "Your neighbour at number 12 says a swarm is hanging in her apple tree. Go and take it into an empty hive, or tell her to ring the club's swarm list?" Adds a new colony with its date and where it came from.
  - Move words **Note a sick animal** (Note). Card: "Bluebell has left her feed and her milk is down since Tuesday. Do you keep her apart, call the vet, or give her another day?" This is where a sickness begins and today nothing catches it. It gives Calling the vet a trigger and the vet the history.
- **Breeding animals** (living things · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: she waits · outcome: her · runs once, recurs · terms: what you expect, the wait, the remainder). Card: "Scanning shows 12 ewes empty. Put them to the ram again, or mark them to sell?"
  You do this when: You choose which animals to breed and see the year through: matings, births, culling.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Done · End · Pause · Resume]
  You choose which animals to breed from and see the year through: choosing the father animals, artificial insemination, scanning, lambing and births, and culling. You start the season, change the pairings, note each result, bring in a vet or breeder, pass a task on, and finish when the births are in; it repeats each year and pauses between seasons. It is a bet made months ahead, so you note what you expect (lambing rate, price) at the start; hiring a ram or bull for the season is the other way. Births are reported through the records the law asks for. Kept apart from Raising animals (daily care and health); Selling animals (animals you sell, culled or not); and Moving animals (taking them somewhere to breed). It usually starts when the breeding season opens, a scan shows animals are empty, your records show your best animals, or births are due.
  Examples: Choose which animals to breed from your records; Breed hens for the traits you want; Decide what to do with animals that did not take; Prepare for births and report them; Count the spawn and book the nursery pond; Set eggs under a broody hen or in an incubator and note the hatch.
  - Move words **Record a birth** (Note). Card: "Daisy calved in the night, a heifer. The milk has started. Do you want to keep her to raise or put her on the list to sell?" A birth in your herd or flock: mother, how many, sex, alive or dead, any tag. It fills the births report the law asks for and the young animal's papers.
  - Move words **Record a service** (Note). Card: "Daisy was served on the 4th with straw from bull 4021 by the AI man. Do you want to be reminded to watch whether she comes back in heat?" The day a cow is served is the only starting date for her calving, and today it is lost. The technician's card is his signed word.
  - Move words **Record a heat** (Note). Card: "Rosie is showing heat since 6 this morning, and you served her last in March. Do you call the technician for today, serve her yourself, or leave her this cycle?" Her tap or words: which animal, when she saw it. FoodOS counts to the next heat and a service window from her own entries; it never guesses a heat.
  - Move words **Record a scan** (Note). Card: "The vet scanned 24 cows today: 19 in calf, 5 empty. Serve the empty five at their next heat, or ask the vet about them?" A scan result on a bred animal (in calf or lamb, empty, how many, due date), the vet's or scanner's signed word. Due dates, dry-off dates and which animals to serve again are read from it.
  - Move words **Put eggs to hatch** (Note). Card: "You set 100 eggs on 3 November; they hatch around the 24th. Note them?" Eggs put in an incubator or under a hen, with the day and how many; the hatch is then Record a birth, so the hatch rate can be read.
- **Growing trees and vines** (living things, food · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: she waits · outcome: her · runs until ended, recurs · terms: what you expect, the wait, the remainder). Card: "Plant 200 avocado trees that fruit in 4 years, or keep the field in maize?"
  You do this when: You plant and keep trees and vines that bear for years: orchard, vineyard, coffee.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Done · End · Pause · Resume]
  You plant and keep trees, vines and bushes that bear for years: an orchard, a vineyard, a coffee or cocoa farm, olives, dates, nuts, berries. Some you tap for sap every day instead of picking a fruit once a season: palm wine, toddy, maple, rubber. They may be yours or trees you rent for the tapping. It is years before the first crop, so you note what you expect and the card shows what the land could grow meanwhile. You plant, prune, graft, feed, protect, and pick or tap each season, and replace old trees when they decline. Before the pick, you read the sugar or taste the fruit to choose the day. Kept apart from Growing crops (sown and harvested within a season); Looking after land for pay (paid to keep land to a standard); Planning what to grow or feed (the plan, before you plant); and Foraging (trees you did not plant). It usually starts when you plan a long crop, when trees get old, or when a season's pruning, harvest or tapping comes.
  Examples: Plant a long crop; Replace old trees; Prune before the season; Plant a tree anyone can pick from; Pick the crop when it is ripe.
  - Move words **Note the weather** (Note). Card: "A frost warning is out for tonight and your Conference pears are in blossom. Run the fans from 2 a.m., or wait for the cold to reach the valley floor?" Frost, hail, heat or a wet spell you saw, what you did about it, for how long, and a photo of any damage. Any public warning is shown beside it as its own word.
  - Move words **Prune or graft** (Note). Card: "The pruning crew finished eleven rows of the Syrah block today. Log the rows done, and let the crew leader sign the same count?" The rows or trees pruned, grafted, thinned, tied or trained, by whom, and the pay per vine or per row if it is by piece. The crew leader or a pruner signs the same count.
  - Move words **Harvest food** (Note or Done). Card: "You picked the lower block of the cocoa farm today. How many kilos of wet beans came off it, and will you ferment them at home or sell them fresh?" Picking or cutting a crop when it is ready: each picking is a Note with its weights; Done is the last harvest of the crop. Grading is Grading produce; milk, eggs, honey and shearing are words on Raising animals.
  - Move words **Water, feed or spray** (Note). Card: "The label on the copper spray you used on the cherry block says 7 days before picking. Do you pick on Friday for the delivery or hold the round until Monday?" Product, dose, block and weather; the harvest interval starts here. A spray keeps the label's waiting days before picking and before people go back in, so a booked pick or crew is checked against them.
  - Move words **Weed or scout** (Note). Card: "You found leaf rust on 12 coffee trees in the east block. Do you spray now, cut the worst trees, or call the extension officer?" What was found walking the field: pests, disease, weeds.
  - Move words **Sow or plant** (Note). Card: "You planted 120 coffee seedlings in the top block with the society's free stock. How many went in, and how many do you plan to replace in the next rain?" Seed, rate, block and date, each time.
  - Move words **Collect the sap** (Note). Card: "You tapped the 14 palms on the ridge this morning and at dusk yesterday. Do you want to write down the litres you got from each visit?" Tapping is a daily flow, not a harvest, and the trees are often not the tapper's. Palm wine, toddy, pulque, rubber and maple all work this way.
  - Move words **Protect from the weather** (Note). Card: "Frost is forecast tonight and 12 tomato plants went out on Saturday. You have 2 sheets of fleece. Cover them, bring them in, or leave them?" Covering, shading, netting, heating or watering a crop against frost, hail or heat, with what you did, where, when and what it used.
  - Move words **Map your plot** (Note). Card: "A buyer wants to know where this cocoa was grown. Walk the edge of the plot with your phone?" Walking the edge of a plot with your phone to record where it is. A buyer may ask for it as proof of where food grew; it stays yours until you share it. Registering the land is a longer act of its own.
- **Breeding plants and saving seed** (living things · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: she waits · outcome: her · runs until ended, recurs · terms: what you expect, the wait, the remainder). Card: "Your best tomato plants this year set early and resisted blight. Save their seed and mark the line?"
  You do this when: You raise the next plants on purpose, from seed, cuttings or crosses, before anything is sold.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · End · Pause · Resume]
  You raise the next generation of plants on purpose: saving seed from the plants that did best, crossing two parents for a new kind, taking cuttings and grafts, or growing young plants in a nursery to sell on or to plant out yourself. Pick on what you need (earliness, yield, taste, resistance, keeping time), save from many plants rather than a few so the line does not narrow, label every lot with its parent, year and place, and dry and store seed cool, dark and dry, testing a sample for germination before you rely on it. Some crops cross with their neighbours, so they need distance or a barrier; hybrids do not come true from seed. Selling plants or seed is a sale of goods (Selling growing supplies). A new variety is know-how, an idea, which no one owns: you may sell your signed word about who may use it, and your own plants are yours. A plant variety right is a monopoly the state grants (Registering your brand has the same shape); where you live may restrict selling or swapping some seed. Kept apart from Growing crops (raising the food, not the next plants); Breeding animals (animals, not plants); Growing trees and vines (a long crop kept for fruit); and Raising animals (animals, not plants). It usually starts when a crop is ripening and the best plants show, sowing time nears, a neighbour asks for seed, or you want young plants for the next season.
  Examples: Save seed from your best plants; Test seed before sowing; Run a small nursery; Take cuttings from a good vine.

### Keep
- **Cleaning** (space, land and water, equipment · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: nobody · outcome: nobody · runs once, recurs · terms: the remainder). Card: "The chopping board had raw chicken on it. Scrub it with hot soapy water before the salad?"
  You do this when: You clean a space or equipment to your own standard, within what the law asks: a kitchen, a parlour, a van. Not servicing a machine, not guarding it.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Done · End · Pause · Resume]
  You clean a space or equipment to your own standard, within what the law asks: a kitchen, milking parlour, van or stall, on a schedule. Washing up at home counts; so does clearing the kitchen before a festival such as Passover. You start the clean, change the list, note what was done, hand a job to someone, and mark it done; it repeats daily or weekly. Proof of cleaning is Logging food safety checks. Kept apart from Making water safe to drink (treating water); Keeping equipment and places in order (servicing and repair, not cleaning); and Controlling pests (it keeps rodents and insects out). It usually starts when a service ends, after raw meat, at a set time on the rota, or before an inspection or festival.
  Examples: Clean equipment or a work area to the standard your rules ask; Clear the kitchen of foods not allowed before a festival; Clean down a milking parlour after each milking; Clean a stall or van at the end of a trading day.
- **Keeping equipment and places in order** (equipment, space, land and water · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: she waits · outcome: nobody · runs once, recurs · terms: the wait, the remainder). Card: "Code E4 on the dishwasher means a blocked drain pump. Clear it yourself in 5 minutes, or book the engineer?"
  You do this when: You service a machine or mend a place you hold so it keeps working: a mixer, a roof, a fence. Not cleaning, not guarding it from loss.
  Also called: Looking after equipment; Maintaining a place.
  Was: act-e57742e165.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Done · End · Pause · Resume]
  You keep your machines and tools working: service them before they break, check that scales and thermometers are accurate, find out what a fault light means and fix what you can, decide whether to repair or replace, and stop a machine when something is wrong until a named person says it is safe again. You start the job, change the plan, note what you found, hand it on, and mark it done; it repeats on a service cycle. Maintaining a place: You keep the places where food is made, kept or grown in working order: fences, drains, roofs, wells, roads, terraces, ponds. This is upkeep, so what you built does not wear away. You plan the rounds, write down what you fixed, bring in help, and it repeats each season. Kept apart from Cleaning (hygiene); Repairing and treating for pay (you pay someone); Keeping people safe at work (the people using it); Keeping the power on (its energy); and Building or improving a place (making something new). It usually starts when a service or check is due, a fault code appears, an alarm trips, or a new machine arrives; as Maintaining a place, when a season changes, after a storm or flood, or when something starts to give way.
  Examples: Book a service for equipment every period; Test and adjust measuring equipment; Decide whether to repair or replace equipment; Stop a machine or line when something is wrong until a person approves it again; Fix it yourself or call someone?; Service a machine by its hours; Mend the nets between trips; Lift the cage nets, wash them and mend holes; Burr kilos and water strip; Act on a fridge drifting before it fails; Act when a vaccine fridge goes out of range; Fix a leak before the rains; Fix what a storm damaged; Keep a shared well in order; Look after fences and drains each season; Drain, dry and lime the pond between crops; Maintaining a place.
  - Move words **Update a machine** (Change). Card: "The milking robot's update changes how it times teat cleaning. Install tonight after milking?" A software update that changes what a machine does needs the owner's yes.
  - Move words **Set up a machine** (Start). Card: "The new oven arrives Tuesday. Book the installer and the first calibration?" Installing, mapping and first calibration.
  - Move words **Report a fault** (Note). Card: "The dishwasher is leaking again. Report it to the landlord with a photo?" Telling whoever looks after it that it's broken or running out.
  - Move words **Note a reading** (Note). Card: "The combi oven shows 4,210 hours. Note the reading?" A meter or gauge read by hand, kept with the time, so use and cost can be worked out later.
  - Move words **Run it by hand** (Note). Card: "The milking robot stopped at 5 this morning and the engineer cannot come before tomorrow. Do you keep milking by hand, or ask your neighbour for a loan of his machine?" A machine taken over by hand is the one moment the machine's own log is blank and her record is the only one. Without it the yield dip, the extra labour and the damage read as mysteries.
- **Keeping people safe at work** (people · changes what it is · alone, none · compelled by a rule · who waits: nobody · outcome: nobody · runs once, recurs · terms: compelled). Card: "The man-overboard drill is due and two crew are new. Run it Thursday?"
  You do this when: You keep the people who work with you from getting hurt: drills, gear, risk checks.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Done · End · Pause · Resume]
  You keep the people who work with you from getting hurt: drills, protective gear, risk checks, training on machinery and chemicals, life jackets, a liferaft, a first-aid kit. You start the check, change what is needed, note what was done, invite the crew, pass it on, and mark it done; it repeats on a calendar and pauses off season. Accidents go to Logging. Kept apart from Keeping equipment and places in order (it is the machine itself, not the person using it); and Keeping the power on (it is the energy, not the people). It usually starts when a drill or inspection falls due, when a new worker joins, when a new machine or chemical arrives, or when the weather turns dangerous.
  Examples: Run drills and checks so no one gets hurt; Check protective gear before a task; Run a fire drill in a kitchen; Show new staff the knives and the fire exit; Spray re-entry and heat breaks for pickers.
  - Move words **Count who came** (Note). Card: "The fire drill is over. Note who took part?" How many people you actually fed or who came, and how many you turned away: the number a scheme, a client or next week's cooking is planned and paid on.
- **Guarding against theft** (food, money, equipment, space, land and water · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: nobody · outcome: nobody · runs once, recurs · terms: the remainder). Card: "The honesty box was emptied twice this month, both after 9pm. Take the cash in at 8?"
  You do this when: You protect stock, cash, kit, crops and animals from being taken.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Done · End · Pause · Resume]
  You protect stock, cash, kit, crops and animals from being taken: an honesty box, shoplifting, stolen machinery, stolen cattle, and marking or tagging what you own so it can be traced. You start the guard, change it as a pattern shows, note what you saw, hand over a watch, and mark it done; it repeats and can pause. A theft that happened is Logging; a gap in the count is found in Checking stock against your records. Losses to foxes, hawks or dogs at night count too. Kept apart from Guarding against scams (tricks, not taking); Controlling pests (animals eating stores); and Keeping money with a bank or wallet (where the money sits). It usually starts when a loss shows in your records, a pattern appears such as empties after dark, new kit is bought, or the season for harvest theft comes.
  Examples: Protect stock, cash, kit or animals from theft; Mark and track machines and animals; Guard a crop before harvest; Watch the ponds at night; Walking the floor: empty packs and a pattern; Examples for harvest and animal theft; Walking the floor: empty packs and a pattern.
- **Keeping food fresh** (food · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: nobody · outcome: nobody · runs once · terms: the remainder). Card: "The chicken's due tomorrow. Move Friday's stir-fry to tonight?"
  You do this when: You keep everyday food good as long as it should be: oldest first, freeze, finish or share.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · Done · End]
  You keep food good for as long as it should be: use what is going off first, freeze or store it, move stock oldest to the front, and decide whether to finish, box up or share what is left. You start, change the plan, note what is used or moved, ask the people you share with, and mark it done. 'Is this still good?' is Checking one food, and the count is Logging. Food that went off and was thrown out is written down as lost, with how much. Kept apart from Ageing food or drink (it gets better as it waits); Guarding against theft (loss to people); Controlling pests (loss to pests); Storing a harvest (a crop held for months); and Cleaning (dirt). It usually starts when a use-by date nears, you bring home a haul, a freezer or power cut threatens, or something is at its peak.
  Examples: Add what you bought to what you have and get reminded before use-by; Freeze or store food so it lasts; Move stock to where it is kept, oldest to the front; Keep an emergency food store for power cuts or storms, and use and replace it before it dates; Decide whether to finish it, box it up or share it; The date walk; The ripening room; Held at the port; The date walk; Use eggs by the day they were laid; Decide on a cask once it is open.
- **Putting money aside** (money · changes what it is · alone, none · by consent · who waits: she waits · outcome: nobody · runs until ended, recurs · terms: the wait). Card: "You earned $620 from jobs this week. Put your tax share aside?"
  You do this when: You set your own money aside for what is coming: a tax pot, a lean-month fund.
  [Start · Change · Question · Answer · Note · Invite · Pass · End · Pause · Resume]
  You set your own money aside for what is coming, the job an employer used to do for you: a tax pot, a rainy-day fund, a lean-month pot, your own pay drawn from the business. You keep it yourself, in a tin or a pot of your own. You start the pot, change the share by your own rule, note each amount, and end it when it closes. Setting tax aside is not paying it.
